Well, I came because things with my in-laws have gotten… it’s not just family arguing, you know? They make it sound like I’m being difficult, but it’s bigger than that. Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “plotting” if it was just one argument over Sunday dinner or something. Families argue, I know that. But this is different.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.

But what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.

But this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.

And, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.

Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.

I’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.

Like all the things I did for this family, all the years I helped and showed up, suddenly don’t count. And once people at church start turning their faces away, you know, that says something. It’s not just in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any other explanation for why your in-laws and the parish committee are acting this way towards you?\n\nPatient: Maybe there’s some family explanation, people get jealous, people talk… but no, I came here because I knew it wasn’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI mean, I was there for people. I helped, I showed up, I did what you’re supposed to do. And now they act like I’m the troublemaker. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. But I do feel like they’re trying to hurt me in another way, through the church, through the altar and that parish committee.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. But I do feel like they’re trying to hurt me in another way, through the church, through the altar and that parish committee.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.

They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI mean, I was there for people. I helped, I showed up, I did what you’re supposed to do. And now they act like I’m the troublemaker. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. But I do feel like they’re trying to hurt me in another way, through the church, through the altar and that parish committee.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. They make it sound like I’m being difficult, but it’s bigger than that. They’ve been using the altar, and that parish committee—people I’ve known for years—to push me out. Like they want everyone to forget everything I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds, but you don’t understand, I used to help with church events, I kept things together when nobody else would. Like all the things I did for this family, all the years I helped and showed up, suddenly don’t count. And once people at church start turning their faces away, you know, that says something. It’s not just in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any other explanation for why your in-laws and the parish committee are acting this way towards you?\n\nPatient: Maybe there’s some family explanation, people get jealous, people talk… but no, I came here because I knew it wasn’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI mean, I was there for people. I helped, I showed up, I did what you’re supposed to do. And now they act like I’m the troublemaker. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.

Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.

But then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.

And it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.

And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. So no, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.\n\nBut then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If they wanted to, yes, I think they could stop. That’s what bothers me. It isn’t like some misunderstanding that just happened by accident.

They know what they’re doing, with the altar and the parish committee and all those little ways of making me look like I don’t belong there anymore. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were pushing me out, making everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.

And, you know, I used to be the one bringing food, staying late, helping clean up after things… nobody had a problem with me then. So yes, if they wanted to stop, they could. They just don’t want to.

It’s not like that exactly. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. When my mood dropped, I still felt guilty and sad, but I was not hearing or seeing those things anymore.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I stopped being able to keep up with the parish preparations or even manage the house the way I usually do, and my family had to take over the festival tasks. My husband started staying close because I was missing meals, forgetting routines, and not caring for myself reliably.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole crisis has been going on for about two months, since the fight with my in-laws over the Día de los Muertos altar.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The heavy sadness and guilt have been with me for about nine months, though they became much harder to handle after the family conflict.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 60 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. That’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “plotting” if it was just one argument over Sunday dinner or something. Like all the things I did for this family, all the years I helped and showed up, suddenly don’t count. And once people at church start turning their faces away, you know, that says something. It’s not just in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any other explanation for why your in-laws and the parish committee are acting this way towards you?\n\nPatient: Maybe there’s some family explanation, people get jealous, people talk… but no, I came here because I knew it wasn’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI mean, I was there for people. I helped, I showed up, I did what you’re supposed to do. And now they act like I’m the troublemaker. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. I don’t think it’s an exaggeration, no. I know people say, “Oh, maybe you’re taking it too personally,” but this wasn’t just my in-laws arguing with me over some family thing.

Families argue, I know that. But this is different.\n\nThey’re using the altar, the church people, that parish committee—people who know me—to make me look like I’m nothing. Like all the things I did for this family, all the years I helped and showed up, suddenly don’t count. And once people at church start turning their faces away, you know, that says something. It’s not just in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any other explanation for why your in-laws and the parish committee are acting this way towards you?\n\nPatient: Maybe there’s some family explanation, people get jealous, people talk… but no, I came here because I knew it wasn’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI mean, I was there for people. I helped, I showed up, I did what you’re supposed to do. And now they act like I’m the troublemaker. So… no, I don’t think it’s just a misunderstanding.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're in any kind of danger from your in-laws or the parish committee?\n\nPatient: Danger… I don’t know if you mean like someone coming at me in the street. It’s not like that exactly. But I do feel like they’re trying to hurt me in another way, through the church, through the altar and that parish committee.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using all of that to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And once they turn people against you like that, well… that can ruin a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are acting against you without any reason?\n\nPatient: Without any reason? No… they have their reasons, I’m sure they tell themselves they do. People always dress things up nice, especially at church, you know. They smile at the coffee table and then later you hear your name has been passed around like a casserole dish.\n\nBut what I’m saying is, I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All those years, all the helping, showing up, doing what needed doing… and now suddenly I’m supposed to be nobody. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe they don’t say it right in front of me, but you can tell when people have been talking. The way they look at you at church, the way they get quiet.\n\nAnd it’s not just gossip, doctor. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and that parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. All the years I was there, cooking, helping, showing up for people—then suddenly I’m made to look like the problem. That doesn’t come from nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your in-laws and the parish committee might not be against you, or that there could be another explanation for their behavior?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, I really have. I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. So no, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.\n\nBut then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are capable of stopping their actions against you if they wanted to?\n\nPatient: If they wanted to, yes, I think they could stop. That’s what bothers me. It isn’t like some misunderstanding that just happened by accident.\n\nThey know what they’re doing, with the altar and the parish committee and all those little ways of making me look like I don’t belong there anymore. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were pushing me out, making everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd, you know, I used to be the one bringing food, staying late, helping clean up after things… nobody had a problem with me then. So yes, if they wanted to stop, they could. They just don’t want to.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I still believe it. I mean, I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement. I came because I knew my in-laws were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s not just about one Sunday or one comment. You know, I used to help with things, bring food, do my part for the family, and now it’s like they want people to forget all of that. Like I never mattered. So yes, I still think they’re against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are against you might be an exaggeration or distortion of reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s an exaggeration, no. I know people say, “Oh, maybe you’re taking it too personally,” but this wasn’t just my in-laws arguing with me over some family thing.\n\nI came in because I knew they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And I did do things, you know — I showed up, I helped, I gave my time. People remember when it suits them, and then suddenly they act like you were never there.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’m distorting it. I think I’m seeing what they don’t want said out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your in-laws and the parish committee is something that might not be true, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking… like maybe it only feels true because I’m upset. But no, I don’t really think it’s just in my head.\n\nI came here because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some argument with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee too, to push me out. And then everybody starts acting like I haven’t done anything for this family, like all the years I showed up and helped don’t count.\n\nIt’s funny, because people remember you when they need a casserole or someone to stay after and clean, but then when it’s time to stand up for you… suddenly nobody knows anything. So no, I can’t say I think it might not be true. It feels true because it is true.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mistaken? No… I mean, I know people keep trying to make it sound like I got the wrong end of the stick, but I didn’t come here over some ordinary family squabble.

I came because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And I have done things, you know. Years of showing up, helping, bringing food, being there when nobody else wanted to stay late.

They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. That doesn’t happen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the people from the parish committee are actually talking about you behind your back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. So no, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.\n\nBut then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are capable of stopping their actions against you if they wanted to?\n\nPatient: If they wanted to, yes, I think they could stop. That’s what bothers me. It isn’t like some misunderstanding that just happened by accident.\n\nThey know what they’re doing, with the altar and the parish committee and all those little ways of making me look like I don’t belong there anymore. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were pushing me out, making everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd, you know, I used to be the one bringing food, staying late, helping clean up after things… nobody had a problem with me then. So yes, if they wanted to stop, they could. They just don’t want to.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I still believe it. I mean, I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement. I came because I knew my in-laws were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s not just about one Sunday or one comment. You know, I used to help with things, bring food, do my part for the family, and now it’s like they want people to forget all of that. Like I never mattered. So yes, I still think they’re against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are against you might be an exaggeration or distortion of reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s an exaggeration, no. And I did do things, you know — I showed up, I helped, I gave my time. People remember when it suits them, and then suddenly they act like you were never there.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’m distorting it. I think I’m seeing what they don’t want said out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your in-laws and the parish committee is something that might not be true, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking… like maybe it only feels true because I’m upset. Evidence? I mean… I suppose people can keep papers and minutes from meetings and say, “See, nothing happened,” but that doesn’t prove what was really going on.

I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out… and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And you know, when you’ve been the one showing up for years, helping, being there, people don’t put that in evidence, do they? They just take it and then act like it never mattered.

So I don’t know how you prove that with a document. But I know what they were doing.

It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. So no, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.\n\nBut then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are capable of stopping their actions against you if they wanted to?\n\nPatient: If they wanted to, yes, I think they could stop. That’s what bothers me. It isn’t like some misunderstanding that just happened by accident.\n\nThey know what they’re doing, with the altar and the parish committee and all those little ways of making me look like I don’t belong there anymore. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were pushing me out, making everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd, you know, I used to be the one bringing food, staying late, helping clean up after things… nobody had a problem with me then. So yes, if they wanted to stop, they could. They just don’t want to.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I still believe it. I mean, I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement. I came because I knew my in-laws were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s not just about one Sunday or one comment. You know, I used to help with things, bring food, do my part for the family, and now it’s like they want people to forget all of that. Like I never mattered. So yes, I still think they’re against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are against you might be an exaggeration or distortion of reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s an exaggeration, no. Yes, I do. I know how it sounds when I say it all at once, but I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement.

I’m not saying every single person wakes up plotting against me. People have their lives, their casseroles, their committee minutes, all of that.\n\nBut this isn’t just me being sensitive. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some family disagreement with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out. To make people forget what I’ve done for this family, all the years I gave and helped and showed up.\n\nAnd, you know, once the church people start looking at you different, it spreads. It’s like when my sister-in-law used to “forget” to invite me to things, and then suddenly everyone acted like I was the one who pulled away. So yes, maybe they have some explanation they’d give you, but I know what it feels like when you’re being erased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… sad, yes. It’s like after all that energy just drained out of me, I started waking up with this heavy feeling sitting on my chest. Like there’s no joy left in the house, or in me either.\n\nI’ll look around at little things—curtains, dishes, the kitchen table—and it all feels so quiet, like everything’s gone gray. I don’t know if “hopeless” is the exact word, but it feels close some mornings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this sad and heavy?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months, if I’m counting right. It didn’t feel the same every single day at first, you know, some days I could still make myself do the usual things around the house.\n\nBut that heavy sadness, and the guilt too, it’s been hanging around for months. It got much harder after all the family conflict started up. That’s when it felt like it really settled on me, like I couldn’t just shake it off anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you in a way that is targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… targeted, yes, that’s the word. It wasn’t just some normal family argument, like who brings what dish or who sits where at Christmas. My in-laws were using the altar, using the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd people act like church business is separate, but it isn’t. Everyone sees who gets mentioned, who gets thanked, who gets left standing there like she never did anything. I gave years to this family, showed up for them, and then suddenly they’re making it like I don’t matter.\n\nSo yes, I do believe it was aimed at me. Not just generally. Me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to harm you in a way that is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “not based on reality,” no. That’s the part people keep trying to make me say, like I imagined the whole thing.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, all those little announcements and who gets asked to help with what… to push me out. To make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, after all the years of bringing food, helping set up, being there when people were sick—nobody counts that when they decide you’re inconvenient. So no, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about your in-laws and the parish committee being against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would change my mind, honestly. People say, “Well, maybe you misunderstood,” but I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a little argument with my in-laws. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that… to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because years ago I used to help with the food tables, and nobody complained then. Everybody liked when I brought things, when I showed up. But now suddenly it’s like they want everyone to forget what I did for this family.\n\nSo unless they admitted it, I suppose… I don’t see how I’d believe otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your in-laws and the parish committee were against you?\n\nPatient: Before all this? I suppose I wasn’t always thinking about it every minute, if that’s what you mean. There were times I was just… doing what I always did, helping with the family, showing up at church things, bringing food, trying to be useful.\n\nBut then it became clear. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd it’s hard, because you give years to people, and then suddenly you’re the problem. So no, once I saw it, I didn’t really go back to thinking they weren’t against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your in-laws and the parish committee are capable of stopping their actions against you if they wanted to?\n\nPatient: If they wanted to, yes, I think they could stop. That’s what bothers me. It isn’t like some misunderstanding that just happened by accident.\n\nThey know what they’re doing, with the altar and the parish committee and all those little ways of making me look like I don’t belong there anymore. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were pushing me out, making everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd, you know, I used to be the one bringing food, staying late, helping clean up after things… nobody had a problem with me then. So yes, if they wanted to stop, they could. They just don’t want to.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee are conspiring against you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I still believe it. I mean, I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement. I came because I knew my in-laws were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out.\n\nAnd it’s not just about one Sunday or one comment. You know, I used to help with things, bring food, do my part for the family, and now it’s like they want people to forget all of that. Like I never mattered. So yes, I still think they’re against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are against you might be an exaggeration or distortion of reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s an exaggeration, no. I know people say, “Oh, maybe you’re taking it too personally,” but this wasn’t just my in-laws arguing with me over some family thing.\n\nI came in because I knew they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And I did do things, you know — I showed up, I helped, I gave my time. People remember when it suits them, and then suddenly they act like you were never there.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’m distorting it. I think I’m seeing what they don’t want said out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your in-laws and the parish committee is something that might not be true, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking… like maybe it only feels true because I’m upset. But no, I don’t really think it’s just in my head.\n\nI came here because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having some argument with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee too, to push me out. And then everybody starts acting like I haven’t done anything for this family, like all the years I showed up and helped don’t count.\n\nIt’s funny, because people remember you when they need a casserole or someone to stay after and clean, but then when it’s time to stand up for you… suddenly nobody knows anything. So no, I can’t say I think it might not be true. It feels true because it is true.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your in-laws and the parish committee is something that you might be mistaken about?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No… I mean, I know people keep trying to make it sound like I got the wrong end of the stick, but I didn’t come here over some ordinary family squabble.\n\nI came because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out… to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And I have done things, you know. Years of showing up, helping, bringing food, being there when nobody else wanted to stay late.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. People can call it whatever they want, but I know what I saw.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your in-laws and the parish committee is something that could be proven or disproven with evidence?\n\nPatient: Evidence? I mean… I suppose people can keep papers and minutes from meetings and say, “See, nothing happened,” but that doesn’t prove what was really going on.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out… and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And you know, when you’ve been the one showing up for years, helping, being there, people don’t put that in evidence, do they? They just take it and then act like it never mattered.\n\nSo I don’t know how you prove that with a document. But I know what they were doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your in-laws and the parish committee are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I know how it sounds when I say it all at once, but I didn’t come here because of some little family disagreement.\n\nMy in-laws were not just arguing with me. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out… and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And that’s the part people keep brushing past. I’ve been there, I’ve helped, I’ve showed up, and then suddenly it’s like I’m the problem.\n\nAnd you know, churches can look very nice from the outside, flowers and candles and all that, but there are people behind those committees making decisions, whispering. So yes, I still think it’s happening."
